## Versioned PE binding and unified pre-destruction guard — windows-261

Previous260 provided native command6. Wine now resolves BTCpu64IosSetStateClose through its existing arm64ec_redirect_ptr machinery and supplies a PE callback that constructs sized command6 with current PEB and target TEB. FEX setter accepts ABI1/non-null callback, refuses a different rebinding and returns0x261. This is a PE callback path, not a native call to a raw EC export RVA. Actual cross-boundary invocation is not yet guest-qualified.

New AGEPAD_TEST_STATE_RETIRE build option defaultsOFF. Enabled unified DestroyRegisteredThreadState calls AgePadDrainNativeState after ScopedThreadCleanup begins and before ThreadCreationMutex/map erasure or destruction. ThreadCPUArea now retains its owning TEB. Pending status polls the same callback with Sleep(1); only exactzero proceeds. Missing callback/unsupported/error sets a process-scoped blocked flag and never returns into void ThreadTerm or CRT teardown. Post-CRT allocator finalization additionally requires no blocked flag. This is conservative retention; indefinitely pending readers or loader-lock dependencies can stall and are not resolved by this guard.

Actual extracted C++ guard test compiles optionON and verifies pending twice thenzero, incompatible binding rejection, nonreturning error behavior (test intercepts Sleep via longjmp), blockedflag, and source order before locks/destruction. ## Explicit native state-close command — windows-260

## Owner-checked native state-close boundary — windows-259

Previous258 migrated three readers. Audited existing PE/native callback command: version4 validates current PEB, and pending process callbacks take a nonreturning native quarantine path before destructors. This differs from Wine's void pThreadTerm callback; an ordinary returned busy status there is ignored.

Added ios_state_close_for_owner(target_teb,owner) in native signal TU. Acquires exact native identity, compares TEB->Peb while held, then closes only state acquisition. Returns0 for closed/drained, positive reader count for pending, UINT64_MAX for invalid/missing/unknown. Scope cleanup releases native reference on every return. No waiting, freeing, native closure or recycling. Caller must supply an independently validated PEB; this internal helper is not authentication of an arbitrary external caller. No PE ABI/teardown caller is connected yet.

## Independent FEX-state reference foundation — windows-257

Previous256 produced a concrete ordering/contract finding. Added state_access to each native identity, independent of native CLOSED/WRITING/count. ios_state_lease_for_teb owns a native lease first, then acquires a state count in one nonblocking CAS attempt. Failure releases native ownership. Scoped cleanup releases state before native. ios_state_retire requires a held native identity and closes only state acquisition, reporting pending count. Identity publication refuses nonzero state_access, so closure cannot be silently reset. No runtime consumer or FEX teardown bridge uses these helpers yet; this is preparation, not active frame lifetime protection.

## Protected diagnostic scans — windows-252

Pump discovery, orphan/live-stamp scans and lock census now use one captured set of leased native registry entries for the entire diagnostic pass. Partial acquisition failure releases every earlier acquisition and skips the pass. Pump ports/TEBs are local and rediscovered each pass; published beacon values are cleared before attempting capture. A retired/busy entry currently suppresses the whole pass conservatively, rather than producing an incomplete census that could misidentify an absent lock owner.

New actual-helper ASan/UBSan test covers full capture, partial-failure cleanup, retirement pending while held, scope-exit drain and rejection of closed entries. All five native snapshot/lease/request/owned-port/scan tests pass; native Simulator build reports34 succeeded,0 failed. Canonical Madeira patch synchronized and reverse-checked. ## Counted native-reader reference foundation — windows-249

Acquire makes one CAS attempt, snapshots while holding a count and releases on invalid publication; no spin behind an interrupted writer. Release drops count. Retirement closes new acquisition and reports count, or UINT64_MAX during writer publication/invalid index. Publisher only enters from an open zero-reader state, refuses active/retired entries, and clears only WRITING on completion so concurrent retirement closure is preserved. Registration refusal quarantines its native thread rather than overwriting the identity. No ordinary runtime callers acquire or retire these leases yet; this is preparation, not active lifetime protection.

## Actual allocator ownership test rules out blind retired-heap unmapping — windows-233

Previous232turn is progress:16worker actualguest cleanup/barrier pass and precise32MiB+4KiB per additional peak worker retention. Audited rpmalloc heap/span/free-list layout before attempting trimming. heap_release queues a heap without detaching its allocations. page_push_local_free_to_heap counts heap-cached free blocks in page->block_used; remote frees publish to atomic page->thread_free without immediately decrementing block_used. Thus both queue membership and raw block_used counts alone are unsuitable emptiness decisions. Huge allocations of ordinary thread heaps are not added to span_used[PAGE_HUGE] (only first-class heaps track them there), so traversing that list cannot prove the entire heap has no live references. Do not free heap metadata based on an incomplete span census.

Added test-rpmalloc-retired-heap.py compiling the ACTUAL vendored rpmalloc.c directly on macOS under ASan/UBSan. A worker allocates a shared object plus a temporary object, frees only the temporary object, finalizes its allocator thread and exits. Parent joins it, confirms exactly one queued retired heap with unchanged mapped-span total, validates all2048shared bytes, then remotely frees the allocation and verifies deferred-free state while block_used remains positive. Only after all user allocations are freed and the worker is joined does the harness enable whole-allocator unmap_on_finalize and finalize for test cleanup. Test passes/exit0. This proves allocator ownership semantics for host configuration, not Windows/iPad geometry or safe integration into FEX teardown.

Initial host compile/link required stubs for unconditionally compiled Windows diagnostic writes and the disabled allocation-watch hook; only these observation hooks are stubbed, not allocator operations. These are harness portability fixes, not changes to production allocator. No runtime source/container/app/Simulator mutation this turn.

Next implementation must choose either (a) conservative normal-span trimming with explicit live-block/cache/deferred-free accounting while retaining heap metadata for outstanding huge/shared allocations, or (b) final allocator destruction only after all FEX objects, main thread state, callbacks and C++ destructors are finished. Cross-thread frees may still be in flight, so no trim from an unsynchronized queue snapshot. Favor full process lifetime correctness for bank reuse; do not declare32MiB spans dead merely because their creating thread exited. Full DE/device/touch/FPS/authentication/retail multiplayer remains unproved.

Source: rpmalloc_thread_finalize calls heap_release, which moves heap from global_heap_used to global_heap_queue without unmapping. heap_allocate reuses that queue; this explains why normal sequential thread churn can plateau while peak-thread heaps persist in distinct process instances. Default global_config.unmap_on_finalize=0; rpmalloc_finalize only frees all used/queued heaps when enabled. iOS CRT hooks call rpmalloc_thread_finalize but provide no process-finalization hook. These source facts establish a missing process allocator teardown path, not proof every retained byte belongs to rpmalloc.

Next audit whole-process allocator lifetime AFTER worker drain and after all objects using its heaps are destroyed, before resetting/reusing a private bank. Do not unmap an individual dead thread's heap: cross-thread objects can remain live there. Native callbacks, main FEX thread state, C++ destructor order and PE TLS lifetime must be included. Overlap can double-count and is deliberately conservative. Zero only means no tracked other FEX threads/cleanup at that point; caller must have stopped creation and retained context. It is not full native callback quiescence or bank-reuse proof. This export is not yet registered with native code and does not implement a shutdown wait.

Generated production FEX containers/banks now stale relative to230 source and must be rebuilt before deployment.

## Socket-table capacity rejection guarded — windows-225

Previous224turn is progress: source audit identified an independent out-of-bounds lookup and unsafe continuation after socket registration rejection. Implemented bounded atomic slot reservation capped at64; NULL owner rejection consumes no slot. Lookup clamps its count and acquire-loads owner publication; registration release-publishes the owner after initializing fd/exiting. Exit clears the owner atomically. No slot reuse introduced: raw exiting pointers and concurrent callbacks still require lifetime proof.

server_init_process_child now returns (size_t)-1 on registration refusal after closing ONLY the supplied child socket, before any owner-dependent fd receive. wine_ios_child_main checks this sentinel before publishing startup_info_size or loading the PE, restores saved global PEB/argc/argv, and returns to the existing child-thread wrapper. It does not use process_exit_wrapper, whose missing-owner fallback closes the root socket. Existing early-startup TEB/PEB retention is not solved by this guard; no complete teardown claim. Existing process-exit races and missing-owner fallback outside this rejection path remain separate concerns.

New tests/test-proc-socket-capacity.py extracts actual registry helpers and the actual pre-handshake child startup prefix. ASan/UBSan pass: eight rounds of128 concurrent unique registrations yield exactly64 accepted, refused owners absent, correct accepted fd lookup, NULL owner consumes no slot, and a deliberately excessive legacy count cannot cause out-of-bounds lookup. Real socketpair exercise proves the rejected child endpoint closes/peer sees EOF while parent socket data transfer still works. Loader sentinel/restore wiring is source-checked, not guest-executed. This is native helper coverage, not actual retail child-churn acceptance or full Wine startup protocol testing.

Native iphonesimulator library build passes (steam-225-native-build.log). Madeira canonical patch updated for server/loader and full reverse-apply check passes. ## Callret allocation failure checks and Wine propagation implemented — windows-219

Previous turn is progress: external review ingested, verified priorities and gate ledger saved. Implemented the first narrow allocation-failure correction without changing/installing the live215candidate.

CallRetStack::InitializeThread now returns checked bool, zeroes unpublished stack state, stops before pointer arithmetic/ZeroScrub when reserve fails, and releases the reservation on commit failure. Failed-init DestroyThread is a no-op; HandleAccessViolation declines a missing cache. ARM64EC ThreadInit destroys the completed core thread and its explicitly owned segment array, releases emulator-stack allocation, clears CPU-area dispatch/state fields, finalizes CRT TLS, and returns STATUS_NO_MEMORY before map/sweeper/frontend registration. Wine loader now checks secondary-thread arm64ec_thread_init status, leaves loader_section and exits that thread rather than proceeding to guest dispatch. Main-process init already has a separate returned-status path. WOW64's void init consumer now explicitly fail-fast checks the bool; graceful WOW64 failure recovery is not claimed or qualified.

Actual extracted CallRetStack implementation tested with injected reserve failure, injected commit failure and successful init, ASan/UBSan: pass. Checks no scrub/name after reserve failure, one release after commit failure, zero dispatch stack fields and safe failed-init destruction. This unit test does NOT execute ARM64EC cleanup or Wine's guest exit path. No claim of end-to-end recovery yet. FEX arm64ec build and Wine arm64ec ntdll build pass; Wine built with required AGEPAD_SIGNED_WINE_TSD=1. FEX/Wine canonical patches synchronized and reverse-checked.

Remaining work before deployment: package updated FEX/ntdll root/child containers; add controlled guest secondary-thread failure injection; verify parent survives and no partial CPU-area dispatch/null fault occurs. Do not replace live QR session before its pending user attempt is resolved or a new test is explicitly scheduled. Other constructor failures (emulator stack, core CreateThread/LookupCache, segment/frontend allocation) are not comprehensively handled by this narrow change and remain open. Resource exhaustion/lifecycle itself is not fixed.

Files: tests/test-callret-allocation-failure.py; private steam-219-fex-build.log / steam-219-wine-build.log. Full DE/device/touch/FPS/authenticated retail multiplayer remains unproved. ## Reconstructed registers capture a different renderer fault — windows-213

Previous turn is progress: actual diagnostic code built, packaged and launched. Revalidated212host98839 at0:49,1:32 and2:47, with one45second verified wait. No restart. Latest capture has16assigned banks; no bank refusal at that observation, so do not claim recurrence yet.

Diagnostic succeeds on guestTID02b8: guest AV reconstructed R9 plus7 equals the recorded fault address exactly. Same-thread libcef handler/RVA anchor maps guestRIP to libcef.dll RVA0x3344325. Actual binary instruction is `movzwl 0x7(%r9), %r15d`; preceding instructions load a32bit value from R12-minus1 into R9D and addR14. R9-minusR14 is0xffd0d022. The target lies inside a4GiB Wine reservation whose logged protect is0, and the native probe cannot read it. These constrain pointer provenance, but the preceding object's contents and original R12 were not captured. This is a different fault RVA from209; no proof that one deterministic instruction defect explains both. Reconstructed registers are not independent validation of reconstruction correctness.

Important diagnostic discrepancy: supplied AV access=1(write), yet reconstructed x86 instruction is a16bit read. signal_arm64_ios.c around9552–9560 uses a broad bit22 heuristic on native opcodes to infer store direction. FEX MemoryOps.cpp emits LDAPURH/LDAPRH variants for16bit TSO reads, so direction decoding needs qualification against the actual native opcode before blaming a guest write. The current capture lacks that opcode. Next capture it at the already-existing native opcode read, then test the classifier against load/store classes; avoid changing exception direction from guessed x86 intent alone. Wrong metadata is not yet proved to cause the invalid pointer or renderer crash.

Primary-source research resolves exit-code semantics: Crashpad util/win/termination_codes.h defines0xffff7003 as kTerminationCodeNotConnectedToHandler, a dump requested for a client never registered with the handler. This is crash-reporting state, not a diagnosis of OOM or the preceding access violation. Source: https://github.com/chromium/crashpad/blob/main/util/win/termination_codes.h (accessed2026-09-09). Local exact Crashpad revision not independently matched; applying the upstream code identity to this binary is an inference consistent with its value and exception sequence. Do not prioritize enabling crash reporting as a substitute for fixing original fault.

Private evidence: steam-213-observation*.json, steam-213-guestav-excerpt.txt, steam-213-renderer-disassembly.txt. 0x101 isSTATUS_ALERTED, an internal notification status here. GetQueuedCompletionStatus returns true for nonnegative IOSB status; Chromium consequently receives success/zero and reports end-of-stream. This is not evidence of a genuine peer EOF, and the queue/count copies are faithful in this captured chain. Data loss occurs before queue insertion via missing callback result, not during the inspected queue transport.

Why that APC lacks a result is unresolved. Inspected queue_apc, thread_queue_apc, thread_cancel_apc, clear_apc_queue and select previous-APC result handling. Possible paths include queue rejection, cancellation, clearing or missing return. Madeira mach_ios.c send_thread_signal extracts a Mach port and uses __pthread_kill; queue_apc rejects if required signaling fails. This is a concrete next diagnostic, not a proven cause. Next capture queue result, chosen target thread state, cancellation/dequeue/result-store and signal outcome for this specific APC owner/lifetime. Do not mask STATUS_ALERTED or manufacture data; repair callback delivery/lifetime once identified.

## Remove cross-process builtin initialization serialization — windows-132

Previous turn built the connector trace and verified a live wait. Revalidated PID15123: still before useful browser startup, no target records. Native sample identifies register_builtin_classes holding builtin_lock across KeUserModeCallback(NtUserInitBuiltinClasses), whose owner thread waits in NtWaitForAlertByThreadId; three other threads wait acquiring builtin_lock. Actual compiled source is build/win32u-unix/class_ios.c, not upstream class.c. Its shared mutex serializes guest callbacks across all pseudo-processes. The sample establishes the blocking shape, not the full guest lock dependency cycle.

Changed class_ios.c registry from completed(pid,PEB) entries plus global callback lock to stable(pid,PEB,pthread_once_t) entries. Global mutex now only selects/initializes an entry; pthread_once runs after unlock, retaining once-only synchronization within one process and allowing independent processes to initialize concurrently. Existing128-entry capacity and re-registration fallback preserved. No forced success or skipped guest callback. pthread_once supplies platform cancellation semantics; recursive same-process initialization is not newly qualified.

Added tests/test-builtin-process-once.py compiling the actual function with ASan/UBSan and controlled guest callbacks. Process A waits while B must finish; concurrent same-process callers execute once, repeated calls do not reinitialize, and samePID/differentPEB initializes separately. Negative control restores the global callback lock and fails the independence assertion as expected. Native win32u and Simulator app build successfully. Canonical madeira.patch includes the source change and reverse-check passes.

Preserved stalled131 logs and replaced only diagnostic app. First windows-steam-process-class-once run exited root97 during RPC bootstrap with error1726 before Steam; this provides no verdict on class init. Retried same build only after that explicit terminal result. Current windows-steam-process-class-once-retry PID16595 live. It advances beyond prior Chrome-version-only stage to BrowserReady, with936 complete trace records and zero incomplete groups on follow-up. Network-service crashes/restarts persist. No69769ac renderer cleanup records yet, so no connector error classification for that failing renderer is claimed. Trace thunk and closure records on other endpoints are not substituted for the target. Integration advancement is consistent with the fix but does not independently establish that every former wait was caused solely by global serialization.

## File-cache request exit cleanup qualified — windows-113

Previous turn made progress by identifying an orphaned shared fd_cache_mutex. This turn revalidated the stalled Steam PID65306 at its exact installed path, then deliberately replaced the diagnostic app to test a source repair. Sole designated Simulator reused; classic candidate and ref preserved.

Native server_ios.c now registers lexical pthread cleanup handlers around the four file-cache critical sections: APC result retrieval, server_get_unix_fd, NtDuplicateObject and NtClose. Server EOF may call abort_thread/pthread_exit from inside these requests. Cleanup releases only the exiting thread's acquired fd_cache_mutex, and closes a descriptor already removed from the cache by Close/Duplicate. The normal path pops without executing cleanup and retains the existing unlock/close ordering. NtClose initializes fd=-1 before registration. No live foreign-owner unlock, forced success, timeout change or blanket termination safety claim.

New tests/test-fd-request-exit.py compiles the actual NtClose critical section and cleanup function with ASan/UBSan. A substituted server transport injects pthread_exit (the observed EOF mechanism); a negative control removing registration reproduces both orphaned lock and removed descriptor leak. With cleanup, exit, successful request and failed request release the mutex and close the removed descriptor. Other three sites are checked for registration; their full semantics are not exercised by this harness.

## Orphaned shared file-cache mutex — windows-112

Previous status turn was a verified wait: exact Steam host PID65306 remained live. This turn completed two native samples and two read-only LLDB captures, detached/resumed successfully, and checked that only the designated Simulator is booted. No restart or runtime edit.

CrBrowserMain, StackSamplingProfiler, IPC:CSteamEngine and RPC workers repeatedly wait in NtClose/NtDuplicateObject/server_get_unix_fd on the native fd_cache_mutex. The mutex at0x105efbc00 has owner value0xa0b431 (10531889) at offset0x18. The loaded libsystem_pthread disassembly confirms the firstfit owner slot is align_down(mutex+0x1f,8), offset0x18 for this aligned mutex, populated from the pthread thread-id slot. Both samples and LLDB thread inventory lack owner10531889. This is strong evidence of an orphaned shared mutex, not merely slow browser startup.

The preceding runtime excerpt records guest process00e8 terminating its other threads, including a reply-read EOF followed by abort_thread. Another abort caller0x1046332e4 maps to server_call_unlocked+616. Current source takes fd_cache_mutex around server requests in NtClose, NtDuplicateObject, server_get_unix_fd and APC result cleanup. read_reply_data calls abort_thread on EOF; abort_thread ultimately exits the pthread. These sections have no pthread-exit cleanup handler. Thus a server-disconnected guest worker can exit without releasing a mutex shared by surviving guest processes. Exact historical native owner-to-guest tid mapping was not logged, so that final association remains inferred.

Next implement and qualify scoped pthread-exit cleanup for these file-cache critical sections, preserving handle invalidation ordering and without force-unlocking another live thread's lock. First reproduce the early-exit path with the actual relevant source, then run Windows parent/child handle stress and direct self-termination regression before replacing Steam. Audit resource/cache lifetime interactions; a mutex release alone does not establish all termination safety. Do not patch the live mutex or treat browser flags/network warnings as the cause without evidence.

Full adapter API pass remains valid but did not prevent this separate integration stall. No Steam login, actual DE gameplay, physical iPad, touch, FPS or retail multiplayer acceptance. Full objective stays active.

Fetched exact Chromium126.0.6478.183 primary sources sandbox/win/src/sandbox_types.h and target_process.cc from chromium.googlesource.com. Enum65 is SBOX_ERROR_INVALID_TARGET_BASE_ADDRESS. TargetProcess::Create obtains child image base, compares against CURRENT_MODULE(), terminates child and returns65 on mismatch. This fits this shared-address-space runtime's relocated child executables; not evidence of a D3D render failure. Source URLs: https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/tags/126.0.6478.183/sandbox/win/src/sandbox_types.h and corresponding target_process.cc. Copies preserved.

Valve's Steam-for-Linux issue8420 documents -no-cef-sandbox (https://github.com/ValveSoftware/steam-for-linux/issues/8420); it alone does not prove Windows behavior. Exact argument string also appears in the supplied genuine Windows steam.exe and steamclient64.dll. Deliberate next experiment windows-steam-cef-compatibility passes --guest-arg=-no-cef-sandbox through the existing literal-argument mechanism, replacing prior still-live baseline for this measured compatibility test. Must verify generated helper arguments and actual behavior.

This explicitly disables Steam Chromium sandbox isolation; it does not fix address-space isolation or authenticate anything. No fake image-base response, binary replacement, game modification, GPU-disable flag or server response substitution. Native/macOS signing settings unchanged. Same256MiB pool/16banks/runtime flags. Sole designated Simulator reused, classic/private inputs preserved. Full DE/device/retail multiplayer/touch/FPS objective remains active and unfulfilled.

## Initial baseline measurements (historical)

| Experiment | Result | What it establishes |
|---|---|---|
| Source-built FEX ARM64 Simulator static libraries | Pass | CPU translator builds locally after platform diagnostic guards. |
| Actual x64 arithmetic through FEX | Pass twice; guest exit value 42 | One bounded x64 block executes in the designated Simulator using write/execute switching. No Windows APIs. |
| Complete native Wine server reconstruction | Pass; 46 source files | No opaque base server archive required. Archive-local name collisions resolved. |
| Native Wine support, TLS, FreeType, DXMT and LLVM 15 | Build passes | Native dependencies compile for Simulator. Wine TLS handlers initially compiled out; corrected their configuration and relinked the actual implementations. |
| Complete Madeira app | Build and launch pass | Diagnostic shell opens, with zero game presents. No Windows guest launched. |
| Simulator MAP_JIT plus separate writable alias | Fail: kernel protection failure | The device-oriented dual-map allocator cannot simply be reused. |
| Debugger-attached ordinary executable mmap | Fail: EACCES | Attaching LLDB alone does not make this allocation work. |
| LLDB AllocateMemory for executable pages | Inconclusive backend failure: invalid address | No valid allocation obtained; do not count the API's misleading empty error as success. |
| Own probe signed with Mac executable-memory exceptions, before UIKit | Fail: EACCES | Tested real allocation under an actual local signature; adding entitlement keys does not grant this Simulator path. |
| DXBC vertex shader translation and Metal library loading | Pass | A pinned Wine test shader translates through DXMT and is accepted as a Metal library. |
| First real render-pipeline creation | Fail: target OS incompatible | Library loading alone missed the wrong platform metadata. Corrected Simulator AIR/metallib metadata and retested. |
| Translated shader pipeline and offscreen GPU draw after metadata correction | Pass: center RGBA 255,0,0,255; command completed | A Windows DXBC vertex shader executes on Simulator GPU. Fragment shader is native test MSL; not a Windows D3D11 call path. |

At this initial baseline no W0–W7 gate was complete: W0 still included Windows PE rebuild/provenance; W1 required actual Windows x64 execution **and** D3D11 drawing. Later checkpoints supersede that baseline. Device execution, genuine Steam login/installation, DE gameplay, multiplayer, touch and FPS remain unqualified.

## Continuous native FEX checkpoint — windows-003

A local optional write-scope hook now surrounds CompileBlock and ExitFunctionLink. The host harness changes the current thread's JIT write protection only while executing native compiler/linker code, restoring executable mode before returning to generated code. Nested scopes preserve the outer writable period. This is a local Simulator experiment, not an upstream contribution or a device entitlement solution.

Fresh automated runs all passed:

| Guest | Result | Write scopes |
|---|---|---|
| Initial arithmetic block | 42 | 1 |
| 40 increments connected by indirect jumps, then exit | 42 | 41 |
| Conditional backward branch repeated one million times | 1,000,000 | 3 |

The runner checks the single authorized booted device and deletes each old result before launch. Evidence and the inspected final screenshot: `docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-003`. Run `scripts/run-madeira-cpu-probes.py` after rebuilding FEX and the CPU probe. These are single-thread correctness tests, not FPS benchmarks or comprehensive JIT qualification. Invalidation, signal reentry, new threads, and broader block-patching paths remain untested.

**Next architectural boundary:** FEX/Source/Windows/ARM64EC/Module.cpp explicitly defines a separate FEXCore instance in xtajit64.dll. Its CMake target embeds FEXCore_object; Wine loads its exported process/thread, simulation and memory-notification functions in dlls/ntdll/signal_arm64ec.c. Consequently the native host scope hooks do not fix Windows execution automatically. A compiler executing from the same MAP_JIT region cannot turn that region non-executable while it continues compiling. Qualify either a native host translator bridge (including ARM64EC context/thunk and exception semantics), a genuinely supported dual-map strategy, or the full-emulation fallback. Do not simply wire the successful CPU probe into a UI and label it a Windows runtime.

UTM's [5.0.5 release](https://github.com/utmapp/UTM/releases/tag/v5.0.5) still documents iOS DXMT and distinguishes sideloaded JIT from the older-firmware hypervisor route. Its full-emulation architecture remains a fallback experiment; neither performance nor DE multiplayer is inferred from the release notes.

The final hook implementation uses explicit registration before thread creation. An intermediate weak-import approach failed the complete-app link and was replaced. All three fresh probes and the complete Madeira Simulator app build passed after the correction.

## Signed Windows PE execution checkpoint — windows-005

The actual source-built ARM64EC FEX and ntdll DLLs now execute selected functions from ad-hoc-signed Mach-O containers in the designated Simulator. Their original native instruction bytes and PE RVA spacing are preserved. Runtime checks verify executable/non-writable code and writable/non-executable data. No MAP_JIT allocation is used for these DLL tests.

| Actual Windows code tested | Fresh result |
|---|---|
| FEX alias registration and translation | Pass: 19,000 data relocations; translation changes 0x10020 → 0x20020 → 0x30020; duplicate/replacement counts remain one. |
| ntdll RtlInitUnicodeString | Pass: 463 data relocations; ASCII, UTF-16 surrogate pair, and null input. |

Both fresh automated runs pass. Build signatures verify, and the signed immutable prefix matches the source PE bytes exactly. Evidence, manifests, logs, screenshots and source snapshots: `docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-005`. The visible app is `local.agepad.ntdll-container-probe`; these are diagnostic applications, not gameplay.

This tests real Windows-compiled functions, but bypasses full import binding, TLS, CRT initialization and Windows process startup. Calling these selected ABI-compatible native entries does not establish general ARM64EC callback/exception correctness. The separate generated-code JIT pool is still required for x64 translation. Physical-device execution and signing remain untested.

A static layout screen of all 230 bundled DLLs found 221 with no screened obstacle to this specific prefix/data layout. Five plain ARM64 DLLs have relocations in the immutable prefix: d3d11 (256), dxgi (965), ntdll (258), win32u (1,540), winemetal (176). Four have no identified native code: ARM64 apisetschema and ARM64EC Normaliz, apisetschema, tftrace-x64. These are classifications, not 221 compatibility passes. The scan covers bundled inputs, not source-rebuild provenance or dynamic behavior.

### Loader integration findings

- `loader_ios.c` enables the existing `.so` DLL loader only on i386/x86_64. Its `map_so_dll` rewrites/remaps headers and assumes Wine-generated shared-library layout. Enabling the macro on ARM64 is insufficient and could overwrite signed pages.
- `virtual_create_builtin_view` can register an existing mapping, but uses `image_info.base`, which the current helper takes from the PE preferred base. A container adapter must provide the actual mapped base and retain the dyld handle. It must reconcile Wine's protection bookkeeping with actual native and emulated code pages.
- `virtual_relocate_module` temporarily requests write access to all PE sections. The container needs its validated data-only relocation path; calling the existing routine against signed text is wrong.
- `load_ntdll_functions` writes dispatcher/data slots and redirects ARM64EC exports. Those writes need explicit validation against the writable region before actual startup.
- Madeira pseudo-processes expect fresh per-process ntdll/FEX data. Reusing one dyld image across Steam processes would share globals incorrectly; a single-process prototype does not solve Steam process isolation.
- The exception-dispatcher `memcpy` examined in `signal_arm64ec.c` copies original code **into a data backup** to detect later patching; it is not itself a code write. Other patching paths still require audit.

Next experiment: implement an opt-in signed-container loader adapter and prove genuine Wine process initialization, preserving failures rather than skipping imports or initialization. First qualify one process; then address isolated pseudo-process instances and the Windows FEX compiler's native JIT write-scope bridge. W0–W7 remain open. No DE menu, match, FPS, real Steam session or multiplayer result is claimed.

## Integrated Wine loader checkpoint — windows-006

Implemented an opt-in signed ntdll adapter in the **actual native Wine loader**, rebuilt all 30 native support objects and the complete Madeira app, and ran it in a separate Simulator bundle (`local.agepad.signed-startup`). The experiment directly starts Wine/server without requiring the existing failed dual-map allocator; this is an initialization diagnostic and does not qualify operation without generated-code JIT memory.

Measured sequence:

1. Native Wine connects to its real wineserver and maps bundled `hello-x64.exe` (AMD64). This is an upstream diagnostic executable with a recorded hash, not a source-rebuilt or completed guest test.
2. The adapter loads signed ntdll, validates data relocation sites and dispatcher slots, registers its actual dyld address with Wine (`status=0`), publishes the discovered pthread TSD offset, and initializes native/ARM64EC dispatchers.
3. Actual PE loader execution initially crashes in `__wine_dbg_strdup`: an address calculation uses x18=0, producing a destination near 0x3008. Existing fault emulation handles direct x18 loads but misses this derived address.
4. Added an opt-in, ntdll-only `NtCurrentTeb` implementation using `TPIDRRO_EL0` and the published TSD byte offset. Zero offset traps rather than reading an arbitrary slot. The first rebuild did **not** include the fix: configured CPPFLAGS do not propagate into this Windows PE compile rule. Corrected explicit `arm64ec_CFLAGS`; disassembly confirms MRS, TSD-slot load and no x18-based address calculation in this function.
5. With the corrected DLL, the real loader passes this failure and reaches main-executable TLS-slot allocation, early locale initialization, and loading `xtajit64.dll` plus ucrtbase/kernel32/kernelbase. These latter DLLs still use their bundled paths. It faults inside that FEX path and eventually terminates. **Windows process initialization remains incomplete; no hello output or guest-entry completion.**

The signed ntdll split is now 0x88000 after the source change, with 463 DIR64 relocations. Fresh isolated FEX and ntdll regression probes still pass. The startup process was verified absent after the failure; the screenshot shows return to SpringBoard. After regression testing, the visible app is again the ntdll diagnostic. No classic game app or scenario was replaced.

Evidence: `docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-006`, including both failed startup runs, emitted build commands, current patches, DLL manifests and regression results. The initial (ineffective) compile and corrected compile are retained separately. Both source patches pass reverse-application checks against the current source trees.

**Next:** extend the signed-container integration to FEX and its initialization dependencies, including their thread-pointer handling, before wiring compiler write scopes into generated-code JIT memory. General imports, TLS callbacks, exception paths, ARM64EC code bitmap and process-isolated DLL state still require qualification. The current adapter intentionally refuses reuse of its registered ntdll instance and is not a Steam multiprocess solution. All W0–W7 gates remain open.

## Signed FEX and dependency integration — windows-007

The actual Wine loader now registers **five source-built signed PE containers**: ntdll, FEX/xtajit64, ucrtbase, kernel32 and kernelbase. The three additional Wine DLLs were rebuilt from the pinned source, with a shared imported TSD-offset lookup. Their source and container hashes are captured per run. `ios_teb_tsd_offset` is now importable (its existing export was private); ntdll also exports the private native-registration table pointer `agepad_signed_images`.

Measured failures and corrections, in dependency order:

| Observed failure | Correction and subsequent evidence |
|---|---|
| FEX metadata points outside its image after registration | Native and PE loaders both relocated it. Native publishes exact base/size/owner records; PE skips only those already-relocated mappings. All five containers then register and their metadata initializes. |
| FEX TlsGetValue derives a pointer from x18=0 before ProcessInit | Local GetCurrentTEB reads the imported, already-published Wine TSD offset. Fixed a missing CMake dependency on the ntdll import-definition file. The fresh build advances beyond early TLS. |
| ntdll ARM64EC indirect-call check reads a null code bitmap | Signed registration validates relocated CHPE ranges and populates Wine's native-code bitmap. All five images are marked; FEX proceeds through allocator/arena setup. |
| FEX calls ntdll's MZ header | Its lookup table assumed incoming targets were x64 export thunks. Added identity entries for metadata-declared native ntdll code. |
| FEX indexes the lookup table outside its allocation | Assembly read a 32-bit table size with a 64-bit load, including adjacent data; corrected the load width and one-past-end comparison. |

The final fresh run logs FEX's build ID, imports TSD offset 0x8d8, logs a FEXUnixLib handle, initializes trackers and returns `pProcessInit -> 0`. It then enters `ThreadInit`, reaching `createthread-enter`. **This is not a qualified initialization pass:** the same log reports the missing WINE_IOS_JIT_RW/RX pool, and thread initialization fails before the x64 test entrypoint completes.

Current first failure: a kernelbase x64 fast-forward thunk is reached as a native branch from FEX's thread creation path. FEX's replacement CheckCall handles only ntdll and returns other targets unchanged; its old “only ntdll is called” assumption does not cover this path. The target bytes, calling instruction and module identities are saved in `fex-lut-width/runtime.log`. Nested exception failures follow; they are secondary.

Next work:

1. Preserve correct ARM64EC handling for targets outside ntdll, such as delegating them to Wine's original call checker, rather than branching into x64 thunk bytes.
2. Supply a real generated-code MAP_JIT pool and native write-scope bridge for the signed Windows compiler. Do not interpret the upstream `ProcessInit` success return as proof when its own log reports missing pool configuration.
3. Replace/audit FEX's assembly TSD placeholders: the original loader patched hardcoded immediates in writable code aliases; signed text requires source-level handling or preparation before signing. The C++ TLS fix does not cover all assembly paths.
4. Complete a source-built Windows diagnostic and D3D11 call path, then the remaining W-gates. Process-isolated DLL state, real Steam, DE, device performance, multiplayer and touch remain unqualified.

One intermediate run (`fex-early-tsd`) used an old staged container after a link failure; it is explicitly invalidated and retained as evidence of a tooling mistake. The runner now rejects missing/mismatched current PE hashes and mismatched container hashes before installation. Fresh regression tests of the FEX alias functions and ntdll Unicode API both still pass. The full runtime app and all native support objects build; current patches pass reverse-application checks. The current core export audit retains all prior names/ordinals for FEX and ARM64EC ntdll, with one added ntdll export. This is structural evidence, not broad ABI compatibility.

Evidence: `docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-007`. Startup processes were verified absent after failures. Only the designated Simulator was used. The final visible app is the ntdll diagnostic after regression testing; the classic game and private inputs are preserved. **All W0–W7 gates remain open.**

## FEX thread initialization and generated-code boundary — windows-008

FEX now preserves Wine's original ARM64EC call checker and delegates targets outside its ntdll lookup table (including empty lookup entries) to that checker. This resolves the measured kernelbase fast-forward-thunk failure in the fresh corrected run.

The first build did not enable the delegation assembly: C/C++ had FEX_IOS_HOST, but CMAKE_ASM_FLAGS was empty. That run repeated the prior failure and is retained as a failed experiment. Added an explicit `AGEPAD_CHECKCALL_DELEGATION=1` assembly flag and confirmed the emitted CheckCall instructions. This deliberately enables only the tested delegation branch; the broader FEX_IOS_HOST assembly paths remain disabled and unqualified. The former notes about hardcoded assembly TSD placeholders apply when those paths are enabled, not as a claim that they executed in these tests.

The corrected full-runtime run logs:

- FEX ProcessInit returns zero.
- ThreadInit passes thread-state allocation, lookup-cache initialization, decoder, pass manager and JIT-core construction.
- ThreadInit finishes with a populated EmulatorData pointer and gs_cached equal to the Wine TEB; Wine records `pThreadInit -> 0`.
- Entry into generated `EnterEC` at 0x70ffdf0138 fails with kernel protection failure. The actual 16 KiB region has protection 3 (read/write, **no execute**). The pool ledger reports uninitialized, consistent with the missing WINE_IOS_JIT_RX/RW environment earlier in the log.

These initialization return values are bounded progress, **not a Windows guest execution pass**. The x64 diagnostic entrypoint has not completed; DE, Steam, device execution, multiplayer and FPS remain untested. The startup app terminates and returns to SpringBoard. Only the designated Simulator was used; existing game inputs and classic candidate remain preserved.

Next: connect a real Simulator MAP_JIT allocation and native write-scope bridge to the Windows compiler, covering initial dispatcher construction as well as later compilation/linking. Do not supply dummy pool addresses, interpret RW memory as executable, or count the earlier native-FEX probe as integrated Windows proof. Continue auditing the assembly ABI and per-process ownership before any Steam qualification.

Evidence: `docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-008`, with both runs, hashes, build logs, CheckCall disassembly and the updated local FEX patch (reverse-application check passes). All W-gates remain open.


## Integrated Windows x64 execution — windows-009

**First source-owned Windows CPU integration pass, repeated in two fresh launches.** The original Windows diagnostic now executes through Wine and the signed Windows ARM64EC FEX compiler in the designated Simulator. This is beyond the earlier native-FEX harness. W1 remains open because its Windows D3D11 drawing requirement is not met.

The new native Wine service registers an actual FEX unix-call table. Correction to windows-007: the old “Loaded FEXUnixLib” message only established that a handle was returned. Source inspection showed the iOS fallback returned a stub table, and the fork disabled its ordinary helper calls. The new service probes a version/magic response before enabling its allocator and scopes.

The Simulator implementation uses a single 64 MiB MAP_JIT pool, records its Wine VM view and ARM64EC bitmap, and allocates bounded chunks at their real addresses. The signed PE compiler can run while the current thread has JIT writes enabled. Nested write scopes cover initial dispatcher construction, thread creation, compilation and link callbacks; the outer leave flushes instructions and restores execution. Zero DualMap offset is valid in this explicitly verified mode. No writable alias or dummy executable address is used.

The initial run rejected the native probe because of an incorrect compiler platform macro and repeated the prior nonexecutable-dispatcher failure. Correcting the guard to TARGET_OS_SIMULATOR enabled the service. The next run printed the bundled hello program's message and exited with code 42. Because its source is not present at the pinned source path, this observation alone does not close diagnostic provenance.

A new source-owned x64 PE, `port/windows/WindowsCPUIntegrationProbe.c`, built without a CRT, then passed twice:

- One million loop iterations with alternating integer branches and a noinline x64 function; result `f6f443a5bcbcbfb7`, independently computed by the Python build script.
- Windows performance-counter calls returned valid ordered counters; this is not an FPS measurement.
- A newly created temporary file received the checksum, was rewound and read back through Win32 APIs, matched and was deleted.
- Each run emitted all three expected markers exactly once, returned guest exit code 0 and completed the Wine process thread cleanly, with no logged guest exception or missing-pool fatal.
- Fresh JIT addresses differed: 0x126190000 and 0x121c68000. Diagnostic executable SHA-256: `2be9386984535449f460c559df4fb7a770b6ecad1762ceb9013e6e131cb1d76e`.

Limits: the pool is a diagnostic allocator with one PEB owner, monotonic allocation and quarantine on release. It rejects unsupported protection/decommit paths; reuse, code-cache remapping, exhaustion, process reentry and Steam multiprocess behavior are not qualified. Cache flushing covers the used pool and is not optimized. Physical-device compilation rejects this service. The existing runtime UI entitlement badges describe the old path, not this verified Simulator service. Its screenshot shows zero presents and zero FPS; no drawing or game performance is claimed.

Evidence: `docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-009`, including all four runs, screenshots, source/executable manifests, CPU verification, native/app/FEX build logs and local patches. Both patches pass reverse-application checks. The final visible app is the separate signed-startup diagnostic after guest exit; the app shell remains alive. Only the designated Simulator was booted. Classic candidate, scenarios and private inputs remain preserved.

Next loop: source-build the Windows ARM64EC DXMT DLLs and required Wine dependencies, extend signed registration only as measured dependencies require, then execute a source-owned Windows D3D11 offscreen draw/readback. Follow with presentation and real DE. No W0–W7 gate is fully closed; real Steam, physical iPad execution, DE, multiplayer, touch and FPS remain ahead.


## Windows graphics reaches Metal; draw remains incorrect — windows-010

**Progress, not a drawing/game pass.** The source-built x64 diagnostic now loads the Windows graphics stack, successfully returns from D3D11CreateDevice with a HARDWARE / feature-level-11_0 request, and passes a blue render-target clear/readback through DXMT and Metal in the designated Simulator. It then issues a real four-vertex draw, but every sampled output pixel remains the clear color. The test correctly exits 34 rather than claiming success.

Source/build changes:

- Built Windows ARM64EC DXMT d3d11, dxgi and winemetal plus Wine gdi32, user32, advapi32, win32u, sechost and msvcrt. Immediate import dependencies were audited separately from delayed imports.
- Added configurable Wine host-tool location and Metal SDK to DXMT Meson; host tools are in build-macos while PE libraries are in build-arm64ec. Embedded command shaders target iphonesimulator. The repeatable `scripts/build-madeira-windows-graphics.py` recipe ran successfully with isolated Meson 1.7.2.
- Extended signed loader registration to those nine measured dependencies. The runner's `--graphics` option checks and stages their containers and matching source PEs, enables the linked Win32u service, and starts from a clean staging app to exclude stale additions. Fourteen signed source-built modules register. Existing imm32 is subsequently mapped by Wine's eager-delay logic; a source-built imm32 container has been produced but was not staged in these observations.
- Added `WindowsD3D11IntegrationProbe.c`, an x64 Windows program using the actual D3D11 API. It uses pinned Wine vertex/pixel DXBC fixtures, tests clear and draw separately, checks 16 interior pixel locations, logs observed mismatches and returns a failing code when appropriate. It neither calls Metal directly nor replaces the runtime renderer.

Measured failures and decisions:

1. Initial imports with uppercase names such as DXGI.DLL failed, despite the installed files existing. Native instrumentation proved Simulator fstatat rejected the uppercase leaf while a lowercase leaf succeeded. Wine trusted the volume's reported case insensitivity and skipped its fallback directory scan. The local signed-Simulator path now retains Wine's case-insensitive component search after exact lookup failure; explicit Unix paths retain exact-case handling. Subsequent runs load the graphics DLLs.
2. Eager creation of `gs_draw_arguments_marshal` failed Metal validation: rasterization disabled, no fragment shader, no color/depth/stencil formats. Moved GS/TS marshal pipeline construction to first actual use with real error handling. The GS feature remains unresolved; TS was not independently tested. This avoids making ordinary device creation depend on an unused helper. It is not broad geometry/tessellation qualification.
3. After that change, D3D11 device creation and GPU clear/readback succeed. Both test shaders compile, a render pipeline with RGBA8 color format is created, and the native Metal encoder receives triangle-strip type 4, four vertices, one instance, a 64x64 viewport/scissor and nonzero GPU buffer addresses. Draw readback still returns `ffff0000` (blue), where the test expects `ff00ffff` (yellow from interior SV_POSITION values).
4. Recreated staging initially reused the same GPU allocation, so that observation alone could not exclude reuse effects. A second variant kept the old staging texture alive and obtained a different GPU address; the second readback was still blue. Simple reuse of the first readback allocation does not explain the failure. Vertex-buffer contents, shader resource bindings/output, command completion and target writes remain to inspect.

The final CPU regression again passes the million-iteration checksum, Win32 file roundtrip and guest exit 0 with the new loader/native app. The visible separate diagnostic app remains open after this CPU guest exits; no scenario was restarted. Only the designated Simulator was booted; devicectl still reports no physical devices. Screenshots and logs distinguish the diagnostic shell from gameplay. No FPS or game completion is claimed.

Evidence: `docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-010`. ## Ordinary Windows D3D11 drawing passes — windows-011

The procedural-vertex control passed while ordinary vertex-buffer drawing failed. Native tracing then verified the original vertex coordinates and the expected buffer address, stride and length at Metal submission. This narrowed the failure to the argument-buffer path. The iOS allocator had supplied malloc memory to Metal's bytes-no-copy API; the observed address was not page aligned. Apple's [buffer API documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/metal/mtldevice/makebuffer%28bytesnocopy%3Alength%3Aoptions%3Adeallocator%3A%29?language=objc) requires a page-aligned memory region.

The iOS staging-block allocator now lets Metal allocate shared storage, uses the returned CPU mapping and keeps its ownership with the Metal buffer. Non-iOS host allocations retain their existing ownership. The unchanged ordinary Windows draw then passed twice: GPU_CLEAR_READBACK_OK, GPU_DRAW_READBACK_OK, PASS v1 and guest exit 0. These are offscreen 64x64 correctness observations, not presentation or performance tests. The optional AIR text dump interleaves concurrent shader output; that text is not evidence of malformed compiled IR.

Evidence and per-run identities: `docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-011`. The refreshed DXMT patch passes reverse-application checking. Diagnostic logging remains enabled and must be disabled or bounded before performance measurement. Geometry/tessellation coverage, larger workloads and physical-device JIT remain unqualified.

Downloaded files would establish input availability only, not W3 Steam execution on iPad. ## One real Windows child completes — windows-019

PE-side tracing establishes the windows-018 corruption cause. Root RtlSetCurrentDirectory_U strips the extended prefix from Wine's absolute Unix namespace path. The child then resolves the resulting relative path against itself: the current-directory copy grows to 754 bytes for a 520-byte destination and overwrites the adjacent command line. Before-copy and before-cwd parameters are correct; after-cwd parameters are corrupt.

The signed-runtime path now retains the extended prefix for the Wine Unix namespace. A destination-capacity check rejects an oversized directory before copying or replacing the old current-directory handle. The rejection branch itself is not runtime-qualified in this checkpoint. Rebuilt ntdll and all child-bank artifacts with validated hashes/signatures; Wine patch refreshed and reverse-checked.

The real CreateProcess probe passes: root and child current-directory copies are both 386 bytes within 520-byte capacity; child sees the quoted executable plus --child, exits 73, and parent verifies its own static value stayed unchanged, then exits 0. This proves the bounded one-child execution/status/data test. It does not prove all DLL independence, concurrent children, general lifecycle safety, CEF or physical-device execution.

Evidence: docs/artifacts/2026-09-08/windows-019. The designated Simulator is the sole booted device. Observed the original-arguments Steam run (PID 60049). steamsysinfo.exe now starts rather than being refused, creates an x64 headless conhost in bank 2, and Steam starts CEF in bank 3. Any normal nested scope sets a thread-local conservative-flush requirement, so nested compilation retains the whole-region flush at outer exit. Ordinary scopes and dispatcher/backpatch paths remain conservative. Write permissions are restored on both paths. Counters distinguish full flushes from explicit-only completions. Therefore neither ordinary CRT exit nor this bounded headless lifecycle alone reproduces the Steam shutdown failure. Do not implement a global teardown bypass based on the prior hypothesis. Steam-specific state, lifetime ordering and concurrency remain candidates; no causal fix is established.

These are bounded correctness regressions, not proof of every instruction-write path or any gameplay FPS improvement. The option remains off by default.

## Descriptor cleanup off-by-one fixed; Steam retry still active — windows-028

Previous turn made progress through five runtime regressions and a genuine Steam retry. The exact optimized Steam PID 64575 is live at 4m39s. It reaches installation verification, actual webhelper startup and Chromium version/process-singleton logs. Conhost now exits zero, so the previous destructor failure is not universal; no causation from the optimization is claimed. Last sampled counters full=20794/explicit_only=11974 show real conservative-flush avoidance. A two-second native sample still shows 1120 top-of-stack sys_icache_invalidate samples; thread totals include waiting threads and must not be treated as a gameplay-FPS metric. No login yet.

The same runtime exposes a concrete cleanup bug: conhost's fd-cache-release closes fd 167, recorded as sysinfo thread 0040's reply read pipe. Source add_fd_to_cache stores fd+1; get/remove decode with -1, but ios_fd_cache_release incorrectly closes the encoded number. Corrected cleanup to atomically consume the entry, skip FD_TYPE_INVALID error entries and decode fd+1 (including valid fd zero). Noninitial cache blocks are mmap allocations; cleanup now munmaps them instead of calling free. This corrects actual neighboring-descriptor closure; it does not qualify the separate concurrent cache-release/publication lifetime model.

Added tests/test-fd-cache-release.py, which extracts the actual union and cleanup function and compiles them against host pipes and mappings. It validates closure of the intended fd, survival of the adjacent descriptor, ignored cached errors, valid fd zero, successful munmap of the extra block, and idempotent repeat release. Test passes; a negative control restoring the off-by-one fails the expected descriptor assertion. Initial harness attempts caught a signed-loop warning (fixed with unsigned j) and used an unreliable macOS mincore expectation (replaced by recording the actual successful munmap call). An old-source negative-control attempt only hit unused-helper compile warnings; it was not counted. Final arithmetic negative control reaches the assertion and is retained.

Native and app builds pass; Madeira patch refreshed and reverse-check passes. Running Steam still uses the previous installed app without this descriptor fix. Preserve its exact handle while observing; the next controlled retry should include this fix after headless/overlapping-child runtime regressions. No extra Simulator or current-app replacement in this checkpoint. Full DE, physical device, multiplayer, touch and sustained FPS objective remains active and unfulfilled.

## Reentrant tracker teardown gate built; actual Steam still observed — windows-030

Prepared an iOS-only invalidation-notification lifetime gate. The original std::optional tracker is now held in a derived storage type whose destructor sets a separate atomic active flag false before the optional base destroys its value. Existing guarded memory/image/invalidation notification entrypoints test that flag first, before reading the optional or accessing the context. This addresses same-thread reentrant callbacks from FEX's own deallocation after tracker destruction starts. An optional destructor does not promise to clear has_value; querying a destroyed optional is invalid. The change does not skip guest destructors, preserve leaked translator state, or bypass a null call. It also does not solve callbacks that already passed the flag on another thread; process-thread quiescence and unguarded internal consumers remain outside this bounded fix. Prepared --direct-teb-check, off by default. Under AGEPAD_SIGNED_WINE_TSD, arm64x_check_call reads the loader-published per-image flag. The rest of target classification is unchanged. x16/x17 are scratch and the original code overwrites x17 immediately afterward; x11 target, x10 dispatch metadata, arguments and stack remain untouched. Disassembly verifies these emitted branches and loads, but does not establish runtime ABI correctness.

The native loader sets the flag for both root and private-child ntdll after publishing the TSD offset, and rejects an explicit request if initialized exports are unavailable. ## SCM connection and recovered alignment; cancellation crash isolated — windows-037

The status-only preceding turn did not change implementation; this continuation revalidated the live diagnostic, examined full exception traces, and built/ran a new source-owned Windows regression.

Real services.exe connected to the SCM probe (MANAGER_CONNECTED). Crucially, the initially alarming STLR alignment fault was recovered: runtime lines 1612–1616 dispatch 80000002 to FEX and report Handled unaligned atomic, followed by actual rpcss.exe creation. The earlier user-facing suggestion that this alignment write itself blocked service startup was incorrect and has been corrected. The old Mach backpatch only recognizes the older alias pool, but the downstream SIGBUS/FEX recovery handles this observed access in the new pool. Do not patch it based on the UNHANDLED Mach label alone.

The later native wineserver fault is real: req_cancel_async+0x158 writes through null x9 (address 8), repeats, and aborts the native wineserver thread. The app shell survived; it was revalidated PID77501 at 14:02:45 UTC before a deliberate diagnostic replacement. No timeout was treated as termination. RpcSs running status and SCM PASS are not established.

Native disassembly maps req_cancel_async+0x158 to list_remove(&async->process_entry) in inlined cancel_process_async, immediately after fd_cancel_async and optional cancellation bookkeeping. The list next pointer is null. This narrows investigation to cancellation/reentrant object and list lifetime; it does not yet prove a use-after-free or justify ignoring null links. Next inspect fd cancellation callbacks, async destruction and tracked-list ownership, then reproduce/repair the actual lifecycle.

Added WindowsUnalignedProbe and reproducible builder: explicit x64 64-bit stores/loads at offsets 0..7, verifying all stored bytes and unchanged surrounding bytes. windows-unaligned-baseline emits BEGIN/PASS and guest exit0. It does not demonstrate forced STLR backpatch at each offset, cross-thread atomicity or comprehensive alignment support. The service trace separately proves its observed STLR recovery. No runtime exception-handling change was made.

## Cancellation use-after-free confirmed in actual service bootstrap — windows-038

Previous goal turn made progress by correcting the recovered-alignment diagnosis and passing a scalar alignment diagnostic. This turn added WINE_IOS/AGEPAD_SIGNED_NTDLL-scoped logging to async destruction and cancellation entry/return; no lifetime semantics were changed. The return log prints pointer identity without dereferencing potentially freed memory. Rebuilt all 46 server inputs and relinked the Simulator app successfully; async.c has no compiler warnings. Canonical Wine patch updated and reverse-apply checked.

Revalidated the previous alignment probe's host PID78369 and its guest exit0, then intentionally replaced the diagnostic with windows-service-cancel-trace. The designated Simulator remained the sole booted device; the classic candidate was preserved. New host PID79442 must be revalidated before further action.

Actual trace confirms the hypothesis: async=0x120f4a580 enters cancellation with refs=1 and non-null next/prev, then async_destroy logs the same address with canceled=1/terminated=1 BEFORE cancel-return logs that address. Immediately afterward req_cancel_async+0x1e8 faults writing address8. This is actual callback destruction followed by caller reuse, not merely an inferred null-list failure. The trace and precise build manifests are retained.

Source chain: default_fd_cancel_async -> async_terminate -> possible synchronous async_set_result -> drop queue reference -> final async_terminate temporary reference release -> async_destroy. cancel_process_async then attaches a cancellation tracker and unlinks the freed process_entry. A repair must retain selected asyncs through callback/list manipulation AND account for completion that occurs synchronously before cancellation tracking. Attaching a tracker after completed async_set_result can leave an uncompletable cancellation group; blindly adding a reference is insufficient. Investigate completed/signaled state and cancellation group ownership with a focused regression before changing semantics. Do not skip null links or return fabricated service success.

SCM connectivity was again reached; no RPCSS_RUNNING/PASS, Steam login, DE gameplay, device performance or multiplayer acceptance. Full objective remains active.


## Cancellation lifetime repair passes actual failure site — windows-039

Previous goal turn confirmed the cancellation UAF with native instrumentation. This turn changes cancel_process_async: retain each selected async before callback, move its process node to the tracked list before reentrant cancellation, attach completion tracking before the callback, and release the retained reference after restoring the process list. A temporary count sentinel keeps the cancellation group alive while synchronous completions occur during construction. Remove the sentinel before deciding whether a wait handle is needed. No null-link skip, fabricated completion or leaked lifetime substitute.

New tests/test-async-cancel-lifetime.py extracts actual cancel_process_async and async_complete_cancel into an AddressSanitizer/UBSan harness with modeled queue ownership and completion. It covers final-reference synchronous completion with/without a cancellation group, mixed deferred/immediate completion and wait-group signaling/release, callback removal of a sibling, and empty selection. All pass with no outstanding modeled refs. Running the same test against saved windows-038 source fails with an AddressSanitizer heap-use-after-free, confirming sensitivity. This is a focused lifecycle model, not an entire Wine server test suite.

All 46 native server sources and the Simulator app rebuild successfully. Wine patch updated and reverse-apply checked. Revalidated PID79442 live and deliberately replaced the diagnostic after confirmed server-thread failure, preserving the classic candidate and sole designated Simulator.

Actual windows-service-cancel-fixed (host PID80543, revalidate) passes the formerly crashing site: async=0x1257b9680 enters with refs=2, callback returns, then destructor runs after restoration. No observed req_cancel_async crash. SCM connects but RpcSs fails process_send_start_message's startup handshake; root probe emits FAIL and guest exits84 instead of losing wineserver. This is progress past the confirmed UAF, not RpcSs acceptance. Last RpcSs load logs are native module image notifications. services.c defaults service_pipe_timeout to10000ms and reports this error after its wait; next distinguish timeout from child exit and investigate startup loader progress before changing timing.

## In-process service startup hook passes CPU; genuine Steam retry active — windows-042

Previous turn passed real SCM/RpcSs bootstrap. To avoid adding a launcher process, added an opt-in AGEPAD_SIGNED_WINE_TSD kernel32 BaseThreadInitThunk hook. A Windows environment request is consumed/removed before creating children; a per-image interlocked guard prevents repeated entry. It launches real services.exe detached, waits on Wine's actual __wine_SvcctlStarted event or child exit with a120s bound, closes handles, and calls the application's original entry only on readiness. Failures exit92–95; no success substitution. This starts SCM, not explicitly RpcSs (normal COM/service requests can start it). Disabled unless requested. Runner --bootstrap-services validates required services.exe before mutations and records the option in run.json.

Rebuilt kernel32, signed root container, three child banks; Wine patch updated/reverse-apply checked. The currently capped first-fault logs do not establish the caller. No blanket writable mapping or memmove skip applied. LLDB detached/resumed after inspection.

Generated build.ninja has FEX_IOS_HOST in C++ flags but not ASM flags; assembly currently uses x18 while ntdll's opt-in dynamic-TSD checker is enabled. Simply enabling FEX_IOS_HOST for assembly would introduce an unretargeted0x898 placeholder and other unrelated iOS paths, so do not do that.
